みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 9 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.54％（年率＋2.17％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 9 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.54％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では9月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、7月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、8月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 2

2

モデルによる9 月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、 図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.54％（年率＋2.17％）と、高い伸びを示す結果になった。他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究
所による9月14日時点のナウキャスティングが同＋0.98％（年率＋3.99％）と、大幅プラスを予測して
いる。一方、日本経済研究センターが公表した9月のESPフォーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月2
日～9月9日、 回答者37名） における民間予測値平均は前期比年率＋0.25％と、 「みずほGDPナ ウ 」の 予
測値対比で小幅な伸びにとどまっている。7～9月期実質GDP予測値については、データドリブン予測
値と民間コンセンサスが大きく乖離していると言えよう。 
両者の乖離は、景気判断の違いというよりも、GDP統計上のテクニカル要因による部分が大きいと
みられる。4～6月期には、中東情勢悪化を背景に原油・ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、石油化学
製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、いずれも
GDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％） とプ
ラス成長になったものの、成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。反
対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の下
方圧力がかかる可能性がある。多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしているのは、こうしたテ
クニカルな要因が考慮されているためだと考えられる。なお、みずほ総合研究所でも、7～9月期GDP
は横ばい圏の伸びにとどまるとみている。 詳細については、 服部直樹 「年率＋1.4％と1次速報から小幅
に上方修正（4～6月期2次QE）」（2026年9月8日） を参照されたい。  
■ 景気実態は改善方向。今後民間予測値が上方修正される可能性にも留意 
データドリブン予測はテクニカル要因を反映しにくい傾向がある反面、より景気実態に近い動きを
捉えているとみられる。 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては、
9月中旬までに公表された月次経済指標を使って予測値を2回更新した。 図表3では、予測値の更新過程
と各月次経済指標の寄与度を示している2。予測値上昇の主因は、8月の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

（出所）内閣府「四半期別GDP速報」、東京財団政策研究所「GDPナウ
キャスティング」、日本経済研究センター「ESPフォーキャス
ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 
（注） モデル予測値は、 各時点で知り得るデータを用いて推計した実
質GDP成長率予測値。棒グラフは予測値に対する各経済指標の
寄与度を示す。「みずほGDPナウ」は各経済指標の結果を公表
前に予測しており （モデル予測値） 、 寄与度はモデル予測値と
実績値の乖離幅から計算される。 なお、 各経済指標の確報を受
けて指標が改定された場合も寄与度に反映される

### Page 3

3

通しDI）が高水準となったことである。要因としては、中東危機に起因する供給網混乱の緩和や旺盛
なAI・データセンター関連需要が企業活動を押し上げていることが考えられる。また、鉱工業生産指
数も予測値の押し上げに寄与した。やはり、半導体製造装置などのAI・データセンター関連製品が好
調であるほか、中東情勢悪化の影響で3・4月に落ち込んだ石油化学製品の回復傾向が続いている。 
以上のように、原油やナフサの輸入増、企業の在庫調整といった動きから、GDP統計上の7～9月期
成長率は低い伸びに抑えられるとみている。ただし、「みずほGDPナウ」が示すとおり、足元の景気
実態は4～6月期対比で緩やかな改善傾向にある。総じて企業部門の回復を示す指標が多く、特に鉱工
業生産は8月の生産予測指数（補正値ベース）が前月比＋3.1％となるなど、好調の持続を示唆してい
る。石油化学製品における在庫復元の動きが続く可能性も考慮すれば、民間エコノミストによるGDP
成長率予測値は今後上方修正されるかもしれない。 
次回の「みずほGDPナウ」については、8月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて7～
9月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組むこととし、10月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。中東
情勢悪化やAI・データセンター投資増の影響が入り組む中、ナウキャストによる景気動向の把握はよ
り重要なものとなるだろう。

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

5
 大企業・製造業の販売価格判断DIは前期並みの高水準を維持。企業は積極的な価格転嫁姿勢を継続

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出

 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正

― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇

― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調

― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大
12
ASEAN5：輸出品目の中国製品との競合度（最終財） ASEAN5：ダンピング・越境EC・少額貨物への主な対応
（出所）各国公表資料、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
（注）2025年の中国から各国への輸出と、各国の世界向け輸出の品目構成の類似度を最
終財（資本財＋消費財）で算出。各品目について両者の輸出総額に占める構成比の小

さい方を採用し、表中はその業種別寄与度（Pt）。値が大きいほど品目構成の重なりが
大きく、中国製品との競合度が高いことを示し、市場シェアや価格競争力を直接示すも
のではない。赤のハイライトは15Pt以上、薄い赤のハイライトは5Pt以上15Pt未満。

アジア：足元でAIブームの恩恵に差。台湾は「生産増」、韓国は「価格上昇」
13
（注）実質GDI、交易利得・損失はみずほ総合研究所による試算

実質GDPと実質GDI、交易利得・損失 ：台湾、韓国 電子・半導体産業の生産指数、輸出価格：台湾、韓国

子部品と情報通信機器等の輸出単価指数を2024年の輸出額を用いて加重平均したもの
（出所） 各国・地域統計、CEICより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵
― 台湾ではAI関連財の生産拡大が成長を押し上げ。韓国では交易条件の改善が所得増に寄与
 相違の背景に産業構造の違い
― 水平分業が確立した台湾電子産業が生産面で広く恩恵が波及
― メモリ主体・垂直統合モデルを特徴とする韓国半導体産業は、メモリ需給ひっ迫による価格高騰が経済を押し上げ

台湾と韓国でGDPとGDIの動きに相違

日本：コアCPIは政策補助効果のはく落を主因に加速。その背後で徐々に高まるインフレ圧力
14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ
― 価格転嫁の影響も拡大しており、原油高・円安等のコスト上昇を背景に家事用消耗品、教養娯楽用品等が加速
― 食品ではコメ価格の下落が続いた一方、コメを除いた食品価格は前月から伸び加速。
― 帝国データバンクの10月調査では、年間の食品値上げ品目数が2万品目台に到達。2025年並みの多さに

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 21 日
上週重點訊息
◼ Fed 三年來首次升息，點陣圖顯示年底前再升一碼：Fed 於 9/16 一致決議升息 1 碼，將聯
邦基金利率目標區間提高至 3.75%～4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首次升息。最新利率預測
中，18 名官員有 16 人預期年底前至少仍需再升息 1 碼。最新經濟預測顯示，Fed 上修今明
兩年實質 GDP 成長率並下修失業率預估值，但也預期通膨壓力將比原先估計更為頑強。主席
Warsh 並指出美國經濟、就業、內需與資本投資仍具韌性，金融環境難稱緊縮。

◼ AI 安全疑慮引發半導體股急跌，但科技產業內部仍呈現明顯分化： OpenAI、Anthropic 等
AI 業者高層呼籲放慢 AI 發展速度、強化安全防護，引發市場重新評估 AI 投資成長速度與資
本支出前景，拖累費城半導體指數 9/14 重挫約 5.9%，但軟體股反而上漲。現階段尚不足以
證明 AI 資本支出循環已反轉，資金亦未全面撤離科技產業，而是從估值較高、籌碼較擁擠的
AI 硬體轉向其他科技次產業。
◼ 日本央行決議升息 1 碼，符合市場預期：日本央行 9/18 以 7 比 2 決議將無擔保隔夜拆款利
率由 1.0%調升至 1.25%。此為該行 2024 年來第 6 次升息，符合市場預期，故升息後對風險
資產衝擊有限。BOJ 明言將「繼續升息並調整寬鬆程度」 ，並將審視調整的時點與步調。
市場展望與操作策略
◼ 科技股中期趨勢未見反轉，但短線波動料加劇：儘管 FOMC 9 月升息，點陣圖暗示今年可能
再升一碼，但其對經濟成長率與失業率預期仍朝正向發展，同時股市也止跌上揚，顯示投資

人認為企業獲利成長動能足以抵消利率上揚的負面影響，基本面較佳的科技股仍具資金承
接，惟宜留意股市短線仍可能因地緣政治與經濟數據表現而出現波動。
◼ 中天期美債及投資級債風險報酬相對均衡：Fed 重啟升息且點陣圖暗示年底前或再升息，短天
期公債殖利率直接受到政策利率上修影響 ； 天期過長的債券則持續面臨財政赤字 、 發債供給及
期限溢價上升壓力，利率敏感度偏高。中天期(5~10 年期)美國公債既可取得目前相對較高收
益，亦具適度存續期間，在未來通膨降溫時較有機會受惠於殖利率回落，風險報酬較為均衡。
投資級債亦具較高收益與信用防禦特性，有助平衡投資組合波動。

## Document 101: 7856 Hermes TP8800 UBS 20261008

Source PDF: all report pdf/7856 Hermes TP8800 UBS 20261008.pdf

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

### Page 2

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 2
Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation

thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized
chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry

participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to
OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.

We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:
1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control

modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and
its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'

pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus
handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,

this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our
reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

Source: UBS
We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with
greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry.
We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 5
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 6
of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes

and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.
Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could

gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular
laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing

remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs
fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference

takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing
equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid

bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that
verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt

mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.
 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of

“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches
deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO

testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be
required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's

Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing
equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 9
highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest

engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.
The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a

comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For
probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/

HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within
this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing

solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they
know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.

Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with
H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider

that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,
with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock

benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We
forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Source: LSEG, UBS
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3

shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO
adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,

more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand
for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential

upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.
Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules

and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We
believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see

What's Priced In chart).
HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6
Oct 2026

Source: UBS estimates
Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized

semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 196: First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

## Document 213: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

The bank
for a changing
world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 19.4% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.9%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 10.6% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 4.3%.

 One companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 100.0% reported 
results exceeding analyst estimates.

 One companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 100.0% reported 
revenue exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of October 5, zero companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 18.1% above estimates, which is above the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 19.4%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €156.8B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.3B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Nine of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improv ement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (98. 6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €27.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-71.4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

The estimated revenue growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 10.6%. The STOXX 600 is expected to earn 
revenues of €1495.4B in Q3 2026, compared to €1351.7B in Q3 2025.

Six of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in revenues relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest revenue growth rate for the quarter, while the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest revenue growth rate (43.8%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€310.9B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €216.2B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-15.4%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€1.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €2.1B in Q3 2025.

estimates for Fiscal Year 1.

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

1. GLOBAL INDUSTRY BREAKDOWN
Tech hardware, including Lenovo, Advantech, and HP Inc, rebounded in Q3. This was partly driven by accelerating AI

server demand and the ongoing data center build-out, although continuing memory shortages raised costs for
hardware manufacturers. Cloud and server providers, like Oracle, Dell and HPE, also highlighted strong AI-related
demand. Semis and equipment were the weakest performers. Elsewhere, entertainment and telecoms posted modest
declines. Finally, in Q3 investor attention increasingly shifted to AI agents, with Meta’s Muse, Microsoft’s Copilot and

Salesforce’s Agentforce among the prominent offerings.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Median returns taken. Updated Sep 30.
Total returns for tech sectors over Q3 vs. YTD returns

2. SEMICONDUCTORS
Chip stocks were hurt initially by a reversal of the AI trade and then by rising bond yields. The Philly SOX index fell
sharply in Q3, after its worst monthly performance in July (-21%) since 2008. Concerns about higher oil prices and
rising rates continued to weigh on the sector in Aug and Sep. SK Hynix was among the weakest performers, amid an

earnings disappointment relative to expectations and an unwinding of leveraged positions. Its Nasdaq ADRs began
trading in July. Despite these headwinds, the semis outlook remains supported by AI infrastructure demand. Within
semis, potential long-term beneficiaries include suppliers of AI infrastructure, memory including high-bandwidth
memory (HBM), advanced packaging, photonics and semiconductor equipment.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.

3.1 MAGNIFICENT 7: Microsoft’s earnings rebound
On July 30, Microsoft rose more than 15%, its strongest daily gain since 2008, following results released the previous

evening. The move added ~$450bn to its market cap, the largest one-day increase on record at the time. For context,
that daily increase is larger than the value of any company in Europe (except ASML).

3.2 MAGNIFICENT 7: Market concentration
According to our equity strategists, from end-July to mid-September, tech-exposed stocks within the S&P 500 rose
14% while the remainder declined 3%. This divergence highlights the concentration of market returns in the tech -

exposed sector. In late July, our strategists anticipated a rotation back into technology, which remains underway.

4. REGIONAL BREAKDOWN
After outperforming in Q2, mainland Chinese tech stocks posted large declines this quarter, hurt by July’s sell-off.
The CSI 300 tech series led the declines. Weakness also extended across Asia more broadly, with the KOSPI, Nikkei 225
and Hang Seng Tech down in Q3, driven by a chip sell-off and concerns over further Fed hikes. European tech equities
were more resilient, with the STOXX Europe 600 Tech Index down slightly lower in Q3 but still well above its year-end

level. Within European tech, Infineon underperformed, while Orange illustrated weakness in telecoms outside the index.

## Document 276: Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

As climate risks have a larger impact on supply chains, the
need to ﬁnance resilience-building initiatives is increasing.
Banks have an important role to play by expanding their
ﬁnancing toolkit, while companies with proactive resilience
strategies can gain greater access to capital
Coco Zhang
As climate risks become supply chain risks, ﬁnancing supply chain resilience is
oﬀering opportunities for banks and companies to explore together
Climate and weather-related shocks are a growing source of supply

chain risk. In August, the Rhine River’s water levels hit a record low
since 1880, hindering trade ﬂows across Europe. In 2024, Hurricane
Helene aﬀected global tech and medical supply chains by ﬂooding
key ultra-pure quartz production facilities in the US. The upcoming
“very strong” El Niño could substantially disrupt temperature and
precipitation patterns across the globe next year, putting, for
example, food production and trade at risk.
These events underscore the need to build supply chain resilience.

Extreme weather is now a more impactful part of the supply chain

### Page 2

risk landscape alongside geopolitics and other risks.
Yet signiﬁcant gaps remain. First, while the awareness of supply
chain climate risks is rising, many companies have yet to
acknowledge their ﬁnancial materiality. Second, the uptake of
ﬁnancing solutions has not kept pace with supply chain resilience
needs, despite trade ﬁnance supporting around 90% of global
trade. This leaves ﬁnancial institutions well positioned to expand
ﬁnancing solutions that support corporate supply chain resilience.

Below is how they can do it.
Expanding banks’ toolkit for supply chain
resilience
1. Broaden the use of sustainable ﬁnance
Today, the most common way banks support supply chain
resilience is through sustainable ﬁnance – in other words,
sustainability-labelled debt. However, scaling up this market
remains challenging because supply chain resilience is not a
standalone category under widely used frameworks such as the
International Capital Market Association’s (ICMA’s) Green Bond

Principles and Green Loan Principles. This makes it harder for issuers
to ﬁnance projects under a dedicated supply chain resilience
theme. As a result, there is also limited data on the total volume of
sustainable ﬁnance supporting supply chain resilience. The global
green bond use-of-proceeds allocation data, for example,
demonstrates this challenge.
Global green bond use-of-proceeds
allocation, January-August 2026

### Page 3

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF
But at the same time, this creates an opportunity. Supply chains
are closely linked to many existing sustainable ﬁnance themes.
Projects that strengthen supply chain resilience can often be
ﬁnanced under established categories such as climate adaptation
(e.g. resilient infrastructure and information systems), sustainable
water management (e.g. ﬂood mitigation), circular economy, and
so on. This requires banks to better understand which supply chain

challenges matter most to each client and identify the
sustainability solutions best suited to their needs.
2. Look beyond labelled sustainable debt
The opportunity to ﬁnance supply chain resilience extends well
beyond traditional sustainable debt markets. Here are some
examples:
Supply chain resilience ﬁnance
The traditional supply chain and trade ﬁnance solutions
can be tailored to integrate resilience objectives. For
example, guarantees can support cross-border trade in

climate-resilient goods, while preferential ﬁnancing terms
can reward companies that actively manage supply chain
risks. By incorporating supply chain risk assessments into
ﬁnancing programmes and linking working capital
solutions to supplier resilience, these products can help
companies better withstand disruptions and support long-
term growth.
Climate and resilience tech ﬁnance

### Page 4

Banks can provide ﬁnancing and advisory services to help
scale and deploy technologies that strengthen supply
chain resilience. Examples include water management
technologies, regenerative agriculture, and indoor farming.
These solutions can improve the upstream supply chains
of industries such as food, beverage, and hospitality. As
interest in resilience technologies grows, ﬁnancial
institutions are increasingly developing tailored ﬁnancing
solutions to support their adoption.

Blended ﬁnance
Some resilience technologies, projects, and markets may
be too risky for commercial banks to ﬁnance on their own.
Blended ﬁnance oﬀers a compelling solution, as it
combines private capital with support from multilateral
development banks or other public institutions. This can
reduce risk for lenders while helping companies access
more aﬀordable funding for resilience-related investments.
For banks, these solutions reﬂect a shift in mindset and strategy.

Moving beyond ﬁnancing individual transactions, banks now play
an important role in helping build a resilient ecosystem of suppliers,
infrastructure, and logistics networks.
Corporates have a part to play too

As banks work to expand their ﬁnancing oﬀerings to enhance
supply chain resilience, companies would beneﬁt from doing their

part as well. My colleague Rico Luman has written about some best
practices companies can adopt in their day-to-day operations.
These include building up inventory of high-risk supply materials,
diversifying suppliers and transport routes, and improving supply
chain visibility. Financing solutions that link resilience to working
capital, for example, can help companies strengthen these eﬀorts.
Companies can also look beyond day-to-day operations and invest

in longer-term resilience initiatives, such as climate-resilient
materials, water management technologies, and other adaptation
solutions. These investments can help create more resilient supply
chains while opening up additional ﬁnancing opportunities.
Treat climate risks as business risks – and
ﬁnancing opportunities
As climate risks become supply chain risks, resilience needs
ﬁnancing. The available toolkit for ﬁnancing supply chain resilience

oﬀers more potential than what the market is consistently tapping
today. This creates considerable opportunities for banks and
companies to explore together.

interposer production to GFS;

因矽中介層產能不⾜，台積電⾸次合作外包給競爭者 GFS ，這應該算
台積電的主動外溢訂單
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
OCT 9 ∙ PAID

READ IN APP
What is new?
半導體晶圓代⼯領域誕⽣了⼀項具備歷史分⽔嶺意義的競合⼤案，全球晶圓代
⼯⿓頭台積電（ TSMC ）與⻑期競爭對⼿美商格芯（ GlobalFoundries, GFS ）
正式簽署⼀項為期五年、價值  20 億美元， 2028 上半年開始量產的製造供應
協議。根據合約，格芯將在其位於美國紐約州⾺爾他（ Malta, NY ）的旗艦
Fab 8 晶圓廠，專⾨為台積電的  CoWoS 先進封裝⽣態系⽣產最關鍵的「矽中
介層（ Silicon Interposers ）」。 Silicon interposer 做完 RDL 層後與邏輯晶⽚
與記憶體進⾏接合，對⾼頻寬、訊號完整性與封裝良率⾄關重要；它不是最先
進  GPU 邏輯  die ，也不代表  GlobalFoundries 取得  Nvidia ∕ AMD 的先進
節點代⼯。
格芯因此成為全美國境內第⼀座具備量產能⼒、專為先進封裝供貨的本⼟矽中
介層供應基地，徹底解決了台積電亞利桑那鳳凰城  Fab 21 在美⽣產

4nm/3nm/2nm 裸晶後，因美國缺乏先進矽中介層製造⽽被迫將晶圓空運回
台灣封裝或將矽中介層空運到美國的荒謬地緣斷鏈難題。

⾵險即可跨⼊⾼⽑利的  AI 先進封裝關鍵環節。但要是台積電在 2030 年⽤玻璃

在台完成」的強烈地緣政治施壓，建⽴完全在美閉環的  CoWoS 替代供應鏈
（台積電 4/3/2nm 邏輯晶⽚ +GFS 的 Silicon Interposer+Amkor 的  WoS 封

測），同時將台積電美國的產能完全留給最⾼附加價值的晶粒堆疊（ Chip-on-
Wafer ）。
美系  AI 晶⽚巨頭（ Nvidia 、 AMD 、⾕歌）：美國國防與聯邦政府機構採購
的敏感  AI 晶⽚，⾃此可符合「全美在地製造與先進封裝（ 100% On-shore

### Page 3

Packaging ）」標準，合規阻礙完全消除。
EDA 、封裝協同設計、良率分析與量測：  跨廠製造需要更嚴格  PDK 、版圖、
電性、翹曲與缺陷資料交換。
矽中介層， HBM 、 ABF substrate 、先進測試與封裝設備：  Interposer 供
給擴張可帶動可組裝的  GPU/ASIC-HBM 套數，但必須與其餘環節同步。
Negatively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and
why?
台灣⼆線矽中介層代⼯廠（聯電  2303.TW 、世界先進  5347.TW ）：台積電
過去在台灣將部分  CoWoS-S 矽中介層外包給聯電等本⼟夥伴；隨著美系客戶
指定美國在地化⽣產，聯電爭取海外  CoWoS 衍⽣中介層訂單的空間遭到分
流，美國第⼆來源降低稀缺溢價並提⾼客戶議價權。
Our outside-the-box, non-consensus thinking
台積電把關鍵的  CoWoS 中介層交給技術競爭對⼿，是否引狼⼊室？我們的⾮

共識觀點指出，矽中介層本質上是⾼耗⾯積、低電晶體密度的低⽑利率材料。
矽中介層通常使⽤  65nm 甚⾄  90nm ⽼舊製程製造，上⾯幾乎沒有主動電晶
體，純粹是⽤深矽穿孔（ TSV ）進⾏⾼密度⾦屬布線  RDL ，但它卻要霸佔珍貴
的  12 吋潔淨室機台空間。台積電將這種「吃產能、賺⼯錢」的低⽑利環節甩
給急需產能利⽤率的格芯，⾃⼰則全⼒聚焦在單⽚要價  3 萬美元的
3nm/2nm 核⼼運算晶粒與頂層⾼難度鍵合。台積電不是出讓技術，⽽是進⾏
⾼超的微笑曲線資本利潤套利。
⽽且這筆交易的真正戰略價值不僅僅是美國製造，⽽是把先進封裝拆成可外包
的模組。若  TSMC 能標準化  interposer PDK 、測試與良率資料，未來可像前
段  foundry 的多廠調度⼀樣管理封裝供應鏈；這會提⾼總體產能韌性，但也
把競爭焦點轉到跨廠協同、良率  ownership 與責任界⾯。⻑期勝者可能不是
單⼀設備商，⽽是能把  design-to-package 數位線索串起來的  EDA ∕

manufacturing data 平台。
Our advice to investors
我們不該把協議解讀為  TSMC 技術外流或  GlobalFoundries 進⼊最先進邏輯
製程。應繼續追蹤四項指標： 1. 1H28 是否能實際量產、 2. GFS 的  silicon
interposer 良率與產能、 3. TSMC 內部  CoWoS 瓶頸釋放幅度、 4. 是否追加
訂單。 US2bn 若平均分五年為 US400mn ∕年，但估值模型應按  2028 起的爬
坡曲線認列。
這項世紀⼤和解是台積電進⾏地緣政治壓⼒卸載的操作。台積電不僅沒有因此
喪失封裝主導權，反⽽⽤外包中介層的代價，將原本對⽴的格芯轉化為⾃⾝的
利益同盟，並在美國本⼟牢牢築起  CoWoS ⽣態圈。我們維持對台積電⻑期的
正⾯評價並同時看好格芯（ GFS ）的結構性轉變。

### Page 4

2026 年 10 月 5 日 • 產業概況更新

電子零組件

AI 眼鏡 - Meta 新品導入 Muse AI 為全天候對話完美載體

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。

陳玫芬  合格證券投資分析人員  & 
CFP 
Lisa.mf.chen@yuanta.com 
葉建伸 
jasperyeh@yuanta.com

元大觀點 
⧫  
Meta 於 9 月發表四款 AI 眼鏡，包含僅音訊無鏡頭的輕薄新品，以及自
有品牌性價比產品；全面導入 Muse AI 功能，具 Agent 代理能力。 
⧫  
全球各品牌 AI 眼鏡全球銷量將突破千萬支，2026 年及 2027 年為 1304
萬支/1618 萬支，YoY +35% / +24%。 
⧫  
已進 Meta 供應鏈的臻鼎、台郡、瑞昱具優勢，顯示相關的全新、
GIS、亞光均具長期機會。 
Meta 發表新 AI 眼鏡四款，以及一款 VR Glasses 
Meta 於 2026 年 9 月舉行 Connect 活動：(1) Ray-Ban Meta 3 較前代續航
時間由 8 小時增至 9 小時；(2) 新發表的 Ray-Ban Meta Audio 特色在拔除相
機鏡頭，消除社會對偷拍與隱私侵害疑慮，總重 43 克為 Meta 最輕薄 AI 智
慧眼鏡，續航時間增至 12 小時；(3) Meta Glasses 為首款自有品牌智慧 AI 
眼鏡，主打平價與高性價比，249 美元起；(4) 具顯示屏的 Meta Ray -Ban 
Display 維持 799 美元價格，具備 LCoS 微型顯示，維持標配 Meta Neural 
Band 腕帶。此外，Meta VR Glasses 為空間運算分體眼鏡，可作為個人沉
浸式影院，採用 micro-OLED 顯示，晶片、電池、儲存空間全部分離至一個
口袋運算盒（Puck）中。 
全面導入 Muse AI 功能，具 Agent 代理能力 
Meta Connect 2026 的硬體與軟體生態佈局中，以上五款產品的底層 AI 全
由 Muse Spark 模型（最新版本為 Muse Spark 1.3）驅動。新款產品能提供 
Instant（即時回應）、Thinking（深度推理） 與 Contemplating（最深思考） 
三種模式，根據問題自由切換回應速度或推理深度。可用於長篇問答對話 、
第一視角即時行動，具 Agent 代理能力，AI 口語速度及語調可客製化調整。 
AI 眼鏡功能持續拓展，估計全球銷量 2026 年超過 1 千萬支 
AI 眼鏡提供更多便利性 ，規格分三種：1) 音訊提供而無鏡頭；2) 音訊及拍
照錄影功能；3) 加上顯示功能。估計各品牌 AI 眼鏡全球銷量 2026 年/2027
年為 1304 萬/1618 萬支，YoY +35%/+24%。AI 眼鏡主要晶片、電池、無線
傳輸及線路均放置於鏡架；PCB 軟板及晶片角色吃重，而鏡片視覺顯示的微
投光機及光波導均為關鍵，微投光機今年 Meta 持續採 LCoS 方案。已切入
Meta 供應鏈的臻鼎、台郡、瑞昱具優勢，顯示相關的全新、GIS、亞光均具
長期機會。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 10 月 5 日  第2頁，共7頁   
Meta 於 2026 年 9 月 Connect 開發者大會推出 AI 眼鏡 
Meta 新 AI 眼鏡主要為四款，及一款空間運算分體眼鏡，均具 Muse AI  
Meta 於 2026 年 9 月中旬的年度 Connect 開發者大會上推出 AI 眼鏡四款及一款空間運算分體式眼
鏡，將 AI 核心升級為全新 Muse 個人 AI 助理：1) Ray-Ban Meta 3 ：為具拍照及攝影功能第三代產
品，續航力提升 1 小時至 9 小時，價格 449 美元(前代 379 美元)；2) Ray-Ban Meta Audio ：拔除
鏡頭以保護隱私，極致輕量化  (43g) 且續航 12 小時，價格 349 美元； 3) Meta Glasses 
(Adventurer)：脫離雷朋（Ray-Ban）標誌，重僅約 53.8 克，內建 3 向可調式鼻墊，價格 249 美元
起；4) Meta Ray-Ban Display ：採 LCoS 顯示光學透視於單鏡片(豪威 OmniVision 供貨) 配 Lumus 
的幾何反射陣列光波導鏡片；20 度視野及相機觀景窗，具低調提詞、即時翻譯字幕、AR 導航與生
活指引功能；標配肌電信號智慧手環(Neural Band)。 
此外， Meta VR Glasses 為空間運算分體眼鏡：採用 micro-OLED 顯示技術的個人沉浸式影院，
晶片、電池、儲存空間全部分離至一個口袋運算盒（Puck）中。 
會中指出智慧眼鏡是能同時向  AI 全天候對話的最完美硬體載體；由過去的回答問題，升級為一個
具備自主行動能力的「個人超級智能」核心；可用於長篇問答對話 、第一視角即時行動，具 Agent
代理能力，AI 口語速度及語調可客製化調整。

AI 眼鏡 2026 年估銷量 1304 萬支，Meta 為領導廠商 
AI 眼鏡估計 2026 年全年銷量 1304 萬支，年增率 35%，上半年已知銷量為 470 萬支；2024 年
/2025 年銷量 271 萬/964 萬，年增率 168%/256%，為極高速成長年度。Meta 市占率達 70%以上，
為領導廠商，小米眼鏡市占率僅個位數百分比。

相關個股  
AI 眼鏡相關台廠營收占比低但仍值得關注 
由於 AI 眼鏡全球銷量於 2026 年將超越千萬支，相關台廠營收占比不高，惟長期仍有機會成為成長
動能；本中心預期已進入 Meta 供應鏈的瑞昱、台郡、臻鼎因技術具優勢 基本面穩健為推薦標的，
已進入陸系供應鏈的華通亦值得留意。未來市場可望陸續推出具鏡片視覺顯示產品，已與客戶共同
研發的全新、GIS-KY、亞光等均值得留意成長動能。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《商品貨幣受制於強勢美元，人民幣溫和升值》
美元在9月FOMC會議再升一碼之後，美聯儲獨立
性有所增強，加上市場對於美國升息預計至明年
中仍有三碼之多，美元再次走強，DXY站上100。
市場對於做多澳幣有所遲疑，市場頭寸再次轉向
空頭主導，但我們認爲包括金和銅在內的大宗商
品價格的反彈令貿易條件的改善，澳幣在0.71以
下已經有了買入價值。人民幣進入年尾出口商有
結匯需求，惟留意中美利差擴大等因素制約，預
計4Q26人民幣偏向升值但空間溫和。

《投資關鍵-澳洲貨幣政策取決地緣與油價變化》
澳聯儲主席布洛克在 2026年9月22日的最新講話中明
確表示，4.5%的失業率目前“仍然偏緊”，失業率需
要升至4.5%~5.0%之間，才能有效為勞動力市場降溫
並緩解通膨壓力；然隨後公布的 8月失業率由 4.5%升
至4.7%，已落在央行目標區間。不過澳洲8月份CPI數
據，年增由3.5%升至4.0%為澳央9月再度升息的主因 ，
主要受到能源價格漲所致；然整體結構未見失控風險 ，
核心CPI年增率持平於3.6%，扣除扣居住與交通CPI年
增由2.2%降至2.1%，因此12月份會期升息與否取決
於油價能否回落 ， 如 地 緣 趨 緩 配 合 失 業 率 維 持 在
4.5~5.0%區間，澳央12月有暫停升息空間。

另外，觀察CFTC投機頭寸顯示，澳幣投機空頭已經大
量累積，做空倉位正在主導市場 ，短期認爲澳幣應在
0.7~0.71的目標價盤整，更多上行空間應來自於美元
降息預期的重新開啓 ，或更多在明年中期左右 。我們
認爲澳幣的看法仍然中性略為正向，而近期跌破0.7關
卡的價位下，有些許布局空間。
• 我們認爲澳幣短線中性偏正向，
在澳幣短期有所回撤 0.7以下價
位下，可適時加碼澳幣部位。

紐西蘭景氣修復逐步延續，中期復甦動能仍在
《投資關鍵-美元強勢壓抑紐元表現 ，惟中期復甦動能
仍在》
9月份紐元表現主要受外部因素主導 。儘管紐西蘭經濟
數據顯示改善跡象，但油價上升及美國財政赤字憂慮推
動美國長天期公債殖利率升至逾二十年高位，帶動美元
走強及市場重新評估美國利率前景 ，令NZD/USD由月
初接近0.59水平回落至0.56附近，紐元與其他高貝他貨
幣同步受壓。
經濟基本面方面 ，紐西蘭第二季 GDP季增0.2%、年增
2.6%，優於央行預期 ，顯示經濟持續復甦 。企業投資

及AI相關無形資產支出保持穩健，惟家庭消費及零售活
動仍然偏弱，反映內需復甦步伐相對溫和。貨幣政策方
面，市場預期RBNZ將於10月維持官方現金利率不變，
以評估既有緊縮政策的影響；惟若經濟活動及通膨壓力
持續展現韌性，年底前不排除進一步收緊政策的可能。
在主要已開發國家央行政策路徑逐漸分化的背景下，紐
西蘭相對較高的利率水準仍可為紐元提供一定支持，但
短期市場焦點預料仍集中於美國利率及美元走勢。
展望未來一至兩季，我們認為紐元仍有望受惠於國內經
濟成長改善及投資活動回暖，逐步擺脫近期低位。然而，
在美國利率維持較高水準及美元強勢延續的環境下，紐
元上行空間可能受到一定抑制 。因此，我們雖仍預期
NZD/USD 可自目前水準溫和回升，但將第四季目標價
由0.60下調至0.59，以反映美元強勢環境可能維持較長
時間。
• 展望未來一至兩季 ，我們認為
紐元有望受惠於國內經濟成長
改善及投資活動回暖 ，逐步擺
脫近期低位 。然而 ，美國公債
殖利率高企及美元韌性較預期
強勁，料限制其升值空間。

通膨數據回落，6月降息的可能性加大重點摘要 《投資關鍵-政策觀望與美元強勢夾擊 ，加幣延續弱
勢格局》
中東局勢未見緩和，油價及通膨壓力維持高檔，促使
聯準會於9月16日升息25個基點，市場焦點亦由降息
預期轉向利差優勢及避險需求，導致非美貨幣承壓。
在市場風險情緒急轉直下，加幣自 USD/CAD 1.385
附近持續走弱，並一度來到 1.42以上。
加拿大央行料維持審慎觀望立場，市場對其轉向鷹派
的預期仍缺乏數據支持。儘管油價上升可能推升整體
通膨，但剔除能源因素後，加拿大經濟基本面尚未出
現足以支持進一步收緊政策的明顯改善。加上近期市
場已大幅回補加元空頭部位，在下一份就業報告公布
前，預料難有促使加拿大央行改變政策基調的催化劑，
因此貨幣政策前景仍偏向中性。
另一方面，高油價有助提升加拿大能源出口收入，對
加幣形成一定支撐；惟中東地緣壓力和美加貿易不確
定性猶存，料將拖慢加拿大經濟及就業復甦步伐。綜
合上述因素，我們預期加幣短期走勢仍偏疲弱，故將
4Q26美元兌加幣目標價由1.37上調至1.39。

，6月降息的可能性加大重點摘要
• 4Q26人民幣將在結匯需求 、中
美利差擴大等因素拉鋸，預計人
民 幣 走 勢 偏 向 溫 和 升 值 ，
USD/CNY Q4 6.65， 明年Q1
6.55。
《投資關鍵 –人民幣迎結匯需求與中美利差擴大的拉鋸》
人民幣與美元指數過去維持相關性，惟2025年下半年開
啟人民幣中期獨立升值行情。儘管兩者走勢高度相似，
但斜率拉開差距，人民幣升值動能顯著強於貶值方向。
今年以來中國景氣K型化樣態持續，出口景氣能見度高
於內需，累積大量貿易順差所引導結匯需求支持人民幣。
惟留意中國內需疲軟依舊，川習會落幕後，中國官方政
策焦點轉向穩增長與五中全會。除房市救市政策積極推
出外，中國人行宣布下調PSL 利率一碼至1.5%。中期來
看人民幣升值動能或受兩因素制約，首先中國經濟週期
挑戰加深不排除有資金流出壓力。其次，由於市場對美
國聯準會的升息預期加強，與中國寬鬆貨幣政策有所差
異，美中利差恐不利人民幣表態。
總結來看，4Q26人民幣將在結匯需求 、中美利差擴大

等因素拉鋸 ， 預 計 人 民 幣 走 勢 偏 向 溫 和 升 值 ，
USD/CNY Q4 6.65， 明年Q1 6.55。

《投資關鍵 -南非幣短線承壓，利差優勢支撐中期前
景》
南非幣近期承壓 ，美元兌南非幣由月初約 16.1升至
16.7水準，主要受美債殖利率攀升及美元轉強拖累 。
市場重新定價美國高利率將維持更久 ，帶動新興市
場資產普遍承壓 。此外，南非債市外資流出及避險
需求升溫 ，進一步加劇南非幣波動 ，使其表現落後
多數主要新興市場貨幣。
儘管外部環境轉趨逆風 ，南非國內經濟基本面仍展
現一定韌性 。近期零售銷售及消費數據表現穩健 ，
支持經濟溫和復甦 。雖然經常帳赤字 、外部需求疲
弱及油價上升、聖嬰現象（El Niño）帶來的糧食供
給風險 ，可能增加未來通膨壓力 ，但亦提高南非央
行維持偏鷹立場的必要性 。我們認為 ，在內需仍具
韌性及通膨風險升溫下 ，南非央行短期不急於轉向
寬鬆，較高利率環境可望持續提供南非幣利差支撐 。
短期而言 ，南非幣仍將受到美國利率 、美元走勢及
全球風險情緒主導 。若美債殖利率維持高檔 ，南非
幣可能持續面臨波動與資金流出壓力 。然而，相較
其他新興市場貨幣 ，我們對南非幣中期前景略趨正

面。一方面，南非央行偏鷹立場有助維持利差優勢；
另一方面 ，黃金價格回穩 、新興市場外匯波動率處
於相對低位 ，以及穩定的套息交易需求 ，均有利南
非幣表現。

《臉書 Muse 點燃追趕行情，谷歌困於模型商品化
與旗艦空窗》
臉書代理式 AI 產品 Muse 迅速爆紅，推動股價單日
創 13 個月來最佳表現。市場開始將臉書從「AI 資
本支出大戶」重新定位為「具備自有 AI 爆款產品的
平台」。月底臉書乘勝進軍企業 AI 平台。然而，AI
代理競爭仍在初期 ，臉書雖挾分發優勢 ，但變現仍
待觀察。谷歌則在法務與搜尋基本面接連傳出利多 。
然而，OpenAI 與 Anthropic 接連大砍 API 價格導
致 AI 推理商品化疑慮，加上旗艦模型 Gemini 4 仍

遲遲未推出 ，谷歌股價因此持續承壓 。資本支出強
度攀升導致的 ROIC 走弱的核心敘事尚未見到反轉 ，
因此通訊服務仍維持中性看待。
• 9 月臉書推出面向消費端的 AI
代理 Muse，正面回應市場持
續關注的變現疑問。
• 臉書近期也成立企業 AI 平台，
將代理人技術延伸至企業端。
• 臉書成功回應市場對臉書有無
AI 應用產品的疑慮。但投入回
收成效仍待進一步驗證。
• 谷歌整合式廣告業務營運未受
結構性衝擊，搜尋有自 AI 競
爭者收復失土的跡象。
• 但谷歌在 AI 模型層仍面臨 AI
兩大壓力，不利市場信心。

資本支出效率與變現進度。靜候 10 月財報結果，維持中立評等。

2
《臉書 AI 投資變現路徑初具雛形，消費端與企業端
同步開展》
9 月臉書推出面向消費端的 AI 代理 Muse，正面回
應市場持續關注的「臉書能否以自有 AI 產品在 40
億用戶基礎上變現」的疑問。Muse 是能代替用戶
執行比價、訂購、換約等具體任務的個人代理人。
上市後迅速登上美國 App Store 與 Google Play 下
載榜首，印證臉書既有社群入口與用戶關係在 AI 產

品分發上的結構性優勢。Muse 雖為臉書打開訂閱、
交易與導購分潤、廣告業務強化三條潛在變現路徑，
但其成敗仍取決於 Muse 能否延續上線初期的動能、
擴展至美國以外市場，並深入用戶日常使用情境。
同時，Muse 的代理能力正引發平台間的摩擦。亞
馬遜隨即封鎖其代理購物功能，拒絕第三方代理人
直接在其平台上替用戶下單，銀行、保險、旅遊等
仰賴消費者惰性賺取溢價的產業也可能採取類似的
防禦措施，交易分潤路徑能否成立，將取決於臉書
與既有平台的合作或議價能力。
緊接在 Muse 之後，臉書近期也宣布成立企業 AI 平
台，並延攬 MongoDB 執行長 CJ Desai 領導此一新
事業，以圖將代理人技術從消費端延伸至企業端。
臉書在企業市場的最大資產是全球大量中小企業已
用來與客戶溝通的 WhatsApp。在 WhatsApp 基礎
上衍生的自動處理詢價、下單、售後客服的企業 AI
代理應用具有相當的想像空間。不過，本行認為臉
書企業平台執行的不確定性明顯高於 Muse。主要
仍是考量到中小企業的數據品質、客戶信任與資安

風險與消費端截然不同。臉書過去缺乏企業銷售團
隊與服務紀錄，從零建立企業級通路需要時間與額
外投入，或將加重臉書的資本與費用負擔。
因此，本行認為 Muse 僅回應了市場對臉書 AI 投資
變現的第一個問題「臉書有沒有 AI 產品」。第二個
問題「投入何時能回收」，仍有待後續進展驗證。

3
《谷歌核心業務韌性無虞，但 AI 變現面臨商品化與
旗艦空窗雙重逆風》
9 月初聯邦法官駁回美國司法部拆分谷歌廣告技術
業務的訴求，改採行為矯正措施，意味谷歌廣告技
術堆疊得以保持完整，整合式廣告業務的營運模式
不受結構性衝擊，延續上半年 Android 應用商店案
和解落幕後法務風險持續收斂的趨勢。搜尋方面，
業界觀察顯示谷歌搜尋已在 ChatGPT 等 AI 競爭者
挑戰下收復部分使用量，AI Overviews 與 AI Mode

似乎有效留住用戶，緩解了 AI 聊天機器人侵蝕搜尋
入口的疑慮。
然而，谷歌在 AI 模型層面臨兩項壓力。第一是 AI
推理商品化。9 月下旬 OpenAI 推出 GPT-6 Sol 與
Luna 並將 API 價格砍半，Anthropic 亦發表 Opus
5.5，效能比肩前代頂級模型、成本卻降低 40%，並
同步上架三大雲端平台。前沿模型價格快速下探，
意味 Google Cloud 的 AI 推理營收單價與 Gemini
API 的定價空間在資本支出擴張之時同步受壓。價
格下行對使用量的影響動態將是市場在 10 月財報時
的觀察重點。
第二個壓力是旗艦模型的持續空窗。9 月下旬
DeepMind 新任主管暗示 Gemini 4 可望於年底前
推出，意味谷歌在 26Q4 之前將持續缺席前沿旗艦
競爭，但而同一期間 OpenAI 已推出 GPT-6、
Anthropic 推出 Opus 5.5。旗艦模型能力雖不必然
保證應用端的落地成效，但 Flash 等輕量模型與法
律、金融等垂直版本，多以旗艦模型為基礎蒸餾或

衍生，旗艦進度因此直接決定整體模型組合所能涵
蓋的應用場景廣度與深度。在上有閉源前沿模型、
下有低價開放權重模型持續滲透企業市場的夾擊下，
旗艦模型進度落後將持續壓抑市場對谷歌雲在企業
AI 應用的看法與信心。

## Document 362: インド金融政策（26年10月）～3年8カ月ぶりの利上げ、金融引き締め局面に転換

Source PDF: all report pdf/インド金融政策（26年10月）～3年8カ月ぶりの利上げ、金融引き締め局面に転換.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

１．結果の概要：政策金利を0.25％ポイント引き上げ 
インド準備銀行（RBI）は10月5～7日に金融政策委員会（MPC）を開催し、政策金利（レポ金利）
を0.25％ポイント引き上げて5.50％とすることを全会一致で決定した。 利上げは2023年2月以来、3
年8カ月ぶりとなる。これに伴い、常設預金ファシリティ（SDF）金利は5.25％、限界常設ファシリ
ティ（MSF）金利とバンクレートは5.75％となった。また、金融政策スタンスを従来の「中立」か
ら「調整された引き締め（calibrated tightening）」に変更した。概要は以下の通り。

RBIは今回のスタンス変更について、当面は利下げを選択肢から外し、今後の政策対応は追加利
上げまたは据え置きになることを意味すると説明した。 ただし、 連続的な利上げをあらかじめ示し
たものではなく、 今後の成長率 ・ 物価動向、 とりわけ基調的なインフレ率、 物価上昇圧力の広がり、
供給ショックの二次的影響、需要動向などを踏まえて判断する方針を示している。

２．金融政策の評価：景気の強さを背景にインフレ警戒を強める 
今回の0.25％ポイントの利上げは概ね市場予想通りだった。一方、金融政策スタンスを「中立」
から 「調整された引き締め」 に変更したことは、
ややタカ派的な内容となった。当面の利下げを
選択肢から外したことで、追加利上げを視野に
入れた政策運営に転じたとみられる。 
RBIが金融引き締めに転じた背景には、 景気が
堅調を維持する一方、中東情勢の悪化に伴う原
油価格の高止まりやモンスーンの降雨不足など
から、 物価上昇圧力が強まっていることがある。
4～6月期の実質GDP成長率は前年同期比7.8％と
高い伸びとなり、7～8月の経済指標からみても
（図表１）

### Page 2

３．成長率・物価見通し：いずれも上方修正 
RBIは今回、2026/27年度の実質GDP成長率見通
しを前回8月の6.7％から7.1％へ0.4％ポイント
上方修正した（図表2）。4～6月期の成長率が
7.8％と予想を上回ったほか、 個人消費や設備投
資が堅調で、製造業やサービス業の活動も底堅
く推移していることを反映した。 四半期別では7
～9月期を7.2％、10～12月期を6.9％、2027年1
～3月期を6.8％、4～6月期を7.1％と予想してお
り、年度見通しは大幅に引き上げたものの、先
行きの成長率が一段と加速する姿を見込んでい
るわけではない。 
物価については、2026/27年度のCPI上昇率見
通しを前回の5.0％から5.2％へ、 コアCPI上昇率
を4.3％から4.4％へそれぞれ引き上げた。四半
期別のCPI上昇率は7～9月期4.9％、10～12月期
6.0％、2027年1～3月期5.7％、4～6月期5.6％と
予想している。 特に10～12月期にはRBIのインフ
レ目標の許容上限である6％に達する見通しで
あり、今後3四半期のCPI上昇率は平均で約5.8％
と、 インフレ目標の中心値である4％を大きく上
回る。 
先行きの物価についてRBIは、モンスーンの降雨不足やエルニーニョ現象による農産品価格への
影響、 原油をはじめとする国際商品価格の高止まりを主な上振れ要因として挙げている。 景気の底
堅さが続くなかで物価上昇圧力がさらに広がれば、追加利上げの可能性は高まる。一方、供給ショ

## Document 373: ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新興国通貨の耐性

Source PDF: all report pdf/ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新興国通貨の耐性.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

新興国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス　〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026 年 10 月 5 日　全 9 頁

ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新
興国通貨の耐性 
耐性は、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルで比較的高い

経済調査部 シニアエコノミスト 増川 智咲

[要約] 
 2026 年の新興国通貨は、中東危機を背景とする原油高とリスク回避姿勢の強まり、米
国の実質金利上昇に伴う対米金利差の縮小という共通のショックを受けたが、通貨間
のパフォーマンスには差があった。 
 2026年4～6月には、多くの国で交易条件や輸出入数量の動向、サービス・所得収支を
含む経常収支の構造が、通貨の強弱と概ね整合した。ただし、インド、南アフリカ
（南ア） 、トルコでは、経常収支だけでは為替動向を説明できず、資金フローやインフ
レ期待などの金融面の影響を大きく受けた。また、2026 年 7 月以降は、ドル安に加え、
各国の利上げや為替介入等が通貨を下支えしてきた。 
 9 月 16 日の米国の利上げと追加利上げ観測は、新興国通貨にとって再びリスクとなり
うる。今後の耐性を総合すると、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルは、経常収支、外
貨獲得力、直接投資の規模、外貨準備の厚みなどの面で相対的に下支え材料が多い。
一方、インドネシアとフィリピンは厚い外貨準備を有するものの、経常赤字と市場性
資金の変動に注意が必要である。タイはエネルギー高に対して脆弱ではあるが、外貨
獲得力や直接投資、外貨準備の厚みが緩衝材になる見通しである。対象国の中では、
海外借入依存度の高さ、期待インフレの高さ、低い政策信認という点で、トルコの脆
弱性が最も大きい。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

2026 年の新興国通貨：共通ショックの後に鮮明となった国別格差 
2026 年の新興国通貨を取り巻く主なリスクには、中東危機を背景とする原油高とリスク回避
姿勢の強まり、米国の実質金利上昇に伴う対米金利差の縮小、ドル高への転換などが挙げられ
る。ただし、各要因の強さは時期によって大きく変化した。このため、本稿では新興国通貨の
対ドル為替レートの動向を 3 つの局面に分けて整理する（図表 1） 。 
第 1 の局面は、中東危機が生じる前の①2025 年 12 月末～2026 年 2 月 28 日である。この時期
は、米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）の独立性をめぐる懸念や利下げ期待などを背景にドル安が
進み、多くの新興国通貨が対ドルで上昇した。 
第2の局面は、②米国によるイラン攻撃から、4月8日の米国・イラン間の初回停戦発表まで
である。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油価格の上昇と、金融市場におけるリスク回避姿勢の強ま
りを受け、新興国通貨は対ドルで幅広く下落した。 
第 3 の局面は、③初回停戦発表から FRB による利上げ直前（2026 年 9 月 11 日）までである。
ただし、この期間も中東情勢は完全には安定せず、為替市場を動かす要因は変化した。 
本稿では、第 3 局面を 4～6 月期の③-1 と、7 月から FRB 利上げ直前の 9 月 11 日までの③-2
とし、前者については貿易・経常収支、後者についてはドル相場、金利差、資本フロー、政策
対応を軸として検討する。 
図表 1 新興国通貨の対ドル為替レート変化率と米ドル実効レートの変化率（2026 年の期間別）

（注 2）米ドルは、プラスが実効レートの上昇を表す。
